# Canonical judicial cases

One row per unique case in the official SJC/CJB roster. The canonical `case_id` is a zero-padded docket such as `2023-07`; legacy, era-based, and Minutes identifiers are preserved as aliases. See [the taxonomy specification](../docs/JUDICIAL-CASE-TAXONOMY.md) for controlled vocabularies and source rules.

**476 records** · classified 476 · needs review 0 · roster only 0

| `1975-01` | Rev. Harold L. Webb & Rev. Thomas Miller v. Pacific Presbytery | Complaint | Dismissed | Not reached | legacy `1975-1`; Minutes `3-12` | Judicial complaint by Rev. Harold L. Webb and Rev. Thomas Miller against Pacific Presbytery regarding its August 4, 1975 meeting conducted without the constitutionally required quorum of three teaching and three ruling elders. The Committee on Judicial Business determined the actions were irregular but recommended dismissal after Pacific Presbytery met in duly constituted session on September 10, 1975, with proper quorum and ratified all actions from the August 4 meeting. | `BCO 14-4` | `quorum requirement`, `presbytery competence`, `constitutional violation`, `judicial complaint`, `ratification of actions` | Classified | [case page](../cases/ga03_1975__case1.md) |

| `2022-07` | Harrell et al. v. Covenant Presbytery | Appeal | Sustained | BCO 40-5 — important delinquency or grossly unconstitutional proceeding; Factual findings — great deference; clear error | legacy `2022-7` | Seven members of a PCA mission church were tried and censured by a provisional Session for opposing the organizing minister's continuation as permanent pastor. South Florida Presbytery | Appeal | Sustained | Discretion and judgment — great deference; clear error; Constitutional interpretation — independent review | — | TEs Felipe Assis and Carter Brown appealed South Florida Presbytery's decision to divest them without censure after their churches left the PCA and their credentials had not transferred. The SJC sustained the appeal, holding that Presbytery clearly erred in judgment and acted unconstitutionally under BCO 34-10. It reversed the divestitures, restored both ministers to good standing, directed Presbytery to dissolve the former calls, and required a constitutionally appropriate disposition; two specifications could not be decided on the record. | `BCO 8-7`, `BCO 13-2`, `BCO 20-8`, `BCO 21-1`, `BCO 23-1`, `BCO 34-10`, `BCO 38-3.a`, `BCO 46-7` | `divestiture without censure`, `ministerial status`, `dissolution of pastoral call`, `church withdrawal`, `transfer of credentials`, `presbytery jurisdiction` | Classified | [case page](../cases/ga52_2025__2025-12_2025-13.md) |
